# Portfolio OS — Study Content

> **Purpose:** This file is the easy-to-read study catalogue for the Portfolio OS study system.
> It contains the same 558 topics as the JSON source. The app should use `data/study-content.json` as the seed source.

## How to study each topic

For every topic, use this order:
1. Read the simple explanation.
2. Look at the mental model.
3. Understand the example.
4. Try the practice task yourself.
5. Check the common mistakes.
6. Answer the interview check without looking.
7. Mark the topic complete only when you can explain and use it.

## Progress rule

- **Not Started:** You have not learned it yet.
- **In Progress:** You are learning or practicing it.
- **Completed:** You can explain it, build a small example, and handle at least one edge case.
- Revisit difficult topics during revision instead of marking them complete just because you read them.

## Topic catalogue

# Full Stack

## Web Fundamentals

### Internet kaise work karta hai

**Simple explanation**

Internet kaise work karta hai is a topic in Web Fundamentals. First understand what it means and what problem it solves. Then learn how it works, when to use it, and what can go wrong. Do not learn it only as a definition: connect it to a real project and test it.

**Mental model**

User or code → Internet kaise work karta hai → application logic → result → user or next step

**Example**

Example: build a very small feature that uses Internet kaise work karta hai. Start with a normal input, check the result, then test one wrong or unusual input. Write down what happened and why.

**Implementation / problem-solving pattern**

```text
Implementation workflow for Internet kaise work karta hai:
request/input → validate → authorize → perform the operation → handle errors → return result → test
```

**Common mistakes**

Common mistakes with Internet kaise work karta hai: learning the name without understanding the reason, copying code without knowing what it does, and testing only the happy path. Always check an edge case and one failure case.

**Practice**

Practice checklist for Internet kaise work karta hai:
1. Explain it in your own words.
2. Draw a small mental model.
3. Build the smallest working example.
4. Test one edge case and one invalid input.
5. Explain one common mistake and its fix.
6. Compare it with one alternative when relevant.
7. Write a short interview answer.

**Interview check**

Interview check: What is Internet kaise work karta hai? What problem does it solve? Give one simple example, explain what happens internally, mention one failure/security/performance concern, and say when you would use it.

Search is a topic in Backend. First understand what it means and what problem it solves. Then learn how it works, when to use it, and what can go wrong. Do not learn it only as a definition: connect it to a real project and test it.

**Mental model**

User or code → Search → application logic → result → user or next step

**Example**

Example: build a very small feature that uses Search. Start with a normal input, check the result, then test one wrong or unusual input. Write down what happened and why.

**Implementation / problem-solving pattern**

```text
Implementation workflow for Search:
request/input → validate → authorize → perform the operation → handle errors → return result → test
```

**Common mistakes**

Common mistakes with Search: learning the name without understanding the reason, copying code without knowing what it does, and testing only the happy path. Always check an edge case and one failure case.

**Practice**

Practice checklist for Search:
1. Explain it in your own words.
2. Draw a small mental model.
3. Build the smallest working example.
4. Test one edge case and one invalid input.
5. Explain one common mistake and its fix.
6. Compare it with one alternative when relevant.
7. Write a short interview answer.

**Interview check**

Interview check: What is Search? What problem does it solve? Give one simple example, explain what happens internally, mention one failure/security/performance concern, and say when you would use it.

Migrations is a topic in ORM — Prisma. First understand what it means and what problem it solves. Then learn how it works, when to use it, and what can go wrong. Do not learn it only as a definition: connect it to a real project and test it.

**Mental model**

User or code → Migrations → application logic → result → user or next step

**Example**

Example: build a very small feature that uses Migrations. Start with a normal input, check the result, then test one wrong or unusual input. Write down what happened and why.

**Implementation / problem-solving pattern**

```text
Implementation workflow for Migrations:
request/input → validate → authorize → perform the operation → handle errors → return result → test
```

**Common mistakes**

Common mistakes with Migrations: learning the name without understanding the reason, copying code without knowing what it does, and testing only the happy path. Always check an edge case and one failure case.

**Practice**

Practice checklist for Migrations:
1. Explain it in your own words.
2. Draw a small mental model.
3. Build the smallest working example.
4. Test one edge case and one invalid input.
5. Explain one common mistake and its fix.
6. Compare it with one alternative when relevant.
7. Write a short interview answer.

**Interview check**

Interview check: What is Migrations? What problem does it solve? Give one simple example, explain what happens internally, mention one failure/security/performance concern, and say when you would use it.

**Implementation / problem-solving pattern**

```text

1) Understand the input and output.
2) Try a simple/brute-force approach.
3) Find the useful pattern or invariant.
4) Implement the simplest correct solution.
5) Test empty, small, duplicate and boundary cases.
6) State time and space complexity.
```

Classes is a topic in Java Fundamentals. First understand what it means and what problem it solves. Then learn how it works, when to use it, and what can go wrong. Do not learn it only as a definition: connect it to a real project and test it.

**Mental model**

User or code → Classes → application logic → result → user or next step

**Example**

Example: build a very small feature that uses Classes. Start with a normal input, check the result, then test one wrong or unusual input. Write down what happened and why.

**Implementation / problem-solving pattern**

```text
DSA practice for Classes:
1) Understand the input and output.
2) Try a simple/brute-force approach.
3) Find the useful pattern or invariant.
4) Implement the simplest correct solution.
5) Test empty, small, duplicate and boundary cases.
6) State time and space complexity.
```

**Common mistakes**

Common mistakes with Classes: learning the name without understanding the reason, copying code without knowing what it does, and testing only the happy path. Always check an edge case and one failure case.

**Practice**

Practice checklist for Classes:
1. Explain it in your own words.
2. Draw a small mental model.
3. Build the smallest working example.
4. Test one edge case and one invalid input.
5. Explain one common mistake and its fix.
6. Compare it with one alternative when relevant.
7. Write a short interview answer.

**Interview check**

Interview check: What is Classes? What problem does it solve? Give one simple example, explain what happens internally, mention one failure/security/performance concern, and say when you would use it.
